ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e2992	We're now on the 2nd. Question No 30, by Mrs Schittenhelm Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3011	Madam President. Federal Minister! We all know that the tax reform, which has become effective this year, has achieved a clear relief from citizens. As a result, the experts now calculate an additional economic growth of 0.4 percent. The first important steps were taken. You, Mr. Federal Minister, have announced in your budget speech that the abolition of cold progression, i.e. the slender tax increase in wage increases for all tax groups, must be the next step. My question is: 241/M “To what extent are the Austrian taxpayers burdened by the Cold Progression?”
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3041	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3056	Like most countries in the world, Austria has a progressive tax system, and therefore the tax burden is usually not only on the tax system, so that more revenue happens, but also on others. Wherever wages are paid higher than inflation rates, this effect arises. If you look at the tax reduction last year, you can see that we have got under control both retroactively and foresightly this cold progression. Depending on the model you calculate, and depending on how long the periods of the collection are, there is an average annual burden on taxpayers of about €400 million.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3075	Do you have an additional question? - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3093	What model for the relief of taxpayers through the abolition of cold progression do you propose? And can you imagine an automatism?
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3108	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3124	There are many countries that have done this and, in part, defined it in the constitutional order. Switzerland, for example, does this at both a cantonal and a general level. The Swedish do something like that too. I have announced to introduce this model. It has been filed in due time, it is under negotiation. The model provides that we will collect the inflation rate over a certain period of time and then, if this point is reached, automatically relieve all tariff levels at the full level of the collected inflation value – with a single exception, which could be that, if there is a very, very poor development in the area of growth, one can temporarily suspend this automaticism. This model has been proposed and is under negotiation.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3243	Madam President. Mr. Finanzminister, if a shareholder of a GmbH wants to raise a luxury villa with construction costs of €3 million, for example, then he does this not privately, but via his GmbH. If he had the luxury villa built in private, he would have to pay the money in advance and pay €825 000 in our example. However, the partner can save the Kest if he can build the luxury villa from his GmbH. Only condition: He has to buy himself free with a very high rent. It is claimed that the GmbH will now also grant the pre-tax deduction on the construction costs of luxury properties, which in turn would mean several hundred thousand euros of tax credit. This tax-saving model for luxury real estate did not come up with a tax advisor, but the Ministry of Finance. My question: 247/M “Is it planned to implement the free purchase option for luxury real estates in Rz 637 ff KstR 2013 in the area of UstR 2000 in order to enable a pre-tax deduction for luxury real estates?”
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3270	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3285	First of all, the high complexity of this question must be maintained. You have already mentioned it: If this is not duly clarified in economic property, then a so-called hidden distribution at the root of the purchase and production costs borne by the company will arise. The possibility of free purchase mentioned in the request means that the shareholder has the opportunity to make the so-called returns to society, so that there is no hidden distribution at the root and thus the economic property remains with society. The yield shall be determined with the average return on real estate used plus a risk premium. If that does not happen – and there we follow a Judicature of the Administrative Court – then that would not be justified. On your specific question regarding pre-tax deduction: The question is not one too one-to-one, as there must be a corresponding entrepreneurial activity when it comes to the possibility of deducting the tax. Thus, only in the case of market-compliant leases will there be an entrepreneurial activity which would further be entitled to a pre-tax deduction if the statutory conditions were met. In summary, it must be said that the corporate tax rules are not comparable to those in the VAT. It is also not possible to take over the regime from corporate tax law in the field of VAT on sales for European reasons.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3866	Madam President. Federal Minister! Now, in turn, €35 billion for the year 2017 are estimated flat-rate. – For what reasons has it been denied to implement the announced task reform?”
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3894	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e3909	The agreement on financial compensation has succeeded in bringing movement into a very rigid system. We've always said that's not going to be a blow, you can't just put a lever around, but we need an entry. We have therefore fixed the task orientation in two areas. ==References== In January 2018, it will enter into force in the first area, for elementary education, and from 1 January 2018 onwards. January 2019 for the second, compulsory school area. In the future, the funds will no longer be distributed according to the population key, but according to clearly defined qualitative and quantitative criteria. As far as kindergartens are concerned, the questions will be how many children will be cared for, how many opening hours are there, how many groups there are and various other criteria by which this task orientation will be achieved. It shows that the money also follows the performance. In the area of tax autonomy, which I have also announced, a first step has also been taken, in the question of how we deal with the housing contributions. In return, measures were tried to be taken at the federal level. For example, there will be a uniform technical construction regulations throughout the country. In addition, we have installed a benchmark system with the countries under which the Austrian statistical services per population are collected, namely the costs per citizen for services provided by the administration. We have agreed to introduce the so-called "Spending Review" on many topics. Secondly, In the area of health and care, where costs are rising most dramatically, two cost-damaging paths have been developed, with the cost-damaging path starting health to exert itself on the reforms by 3.6 and going down to 3.2. ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e4209	Federal Minister, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e4224	It is right that BEPS-OECD measures, in particular at European level, should be used to limit these tax arrangements, which, to a considerable extent, appear to be legal. Group taxation is an essential component of Austria's location. It has been corrected several times, as you yourself have mentioned, whether this is the enforcement aid, the loss allocation, the loss cover or the fact that only when the economic withdrawal is committed is defined, or the question for which countries this is to be applied. To date, there has been no discussion as to whether this measure is not in conformity. In the view of the BMF, its concrete proposal cannot be implemented. Profits from foreign subsidiaries are not subject to Austrian tax jurisdiction. Neither the national tax law nor the double taxation agreements concluded by Austria provide for a tax right for Austria to benefit from foreign companies. These apply exclusively to the State of residence of the foreign body, which is also in line with the usual contractual practice of the Contracting States. Profits from foreign group members are therefore negligible for the purposes of group taxation from today's point of view. Moreover, the proposed model would lead to a final and uncovered consideration of foreign losses which we have now deliberately restricted, and, unlike the time when loss consideration is only temporary, domestic re-taxation is carried out in case of loss consideration or withdrawal abroad. Complexity is not yet to be said, but I think the group taxation is a good instrument for site policy in Austria. That is why I think it should be maintained as it has now been corrected.

Therefore, we assume that the discharge effect on smaller incomes was stronger. However, we cannot show analytically how the effects are in detail, as not even a year has passed. The cold progression is always in the foreground and in the focus. I believe that it is more useful for the Minister for Finance to draw up budgets to make a permanent relief than to make a major discharge much more difficult to finance. And the second point is: The more purchasing power we bring directly into consumption, the better our economic growth will be. That is why I proposed the model for dealing with cold progression.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e6551	Thank you, Madam President. Mr. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Two things are being done here today: One is to introduce the Central Electoral Register, and the other is small changes to the electoral law which are apparently the first step due to the findings of the Constitutional Court. First of all, to the register of voters: We now have such a central voters register!, that sounds so great. - We're for it. I think that's a contemporary and right thing. But we should not believe that we have really solved all the problems now. We have even solved relatively few problems, we only create better data quality. This is the only really essential thing to ensure that the data can be better aggregated and merged and thus, of course, better verifiability is ensured. We can't do it because, for example, you can go to every constituency and vote there from now on, because you can see through the Central Electoral Register, who has already chosen and who doesn't. We don't create all these things, so we already have to say goodbye to believing that we have solved so many problems. But on the other hand, I have to say that I am glad that this has not resulted in a family database. We have long been opposed to this register of voters because, in our view, it has not been ensured that, through such a register of voters, not all of the measures a voters are taking – for example, support for a party, support for a popular desire, support for a candidate and similar things, or even participation in a election – are stored in a central register and can be accessed. It is possible to recall the attitude of an electorate. That's not the case, you have to hold it. That is why we agree today, but that is why this debate took so long, among other things, because we wanted to ensure it. Now to this discussion on the support of parties: An essential argument for this Central Electoral Register was that in the course of this mini-reform we can make it possible to support popular desires in every community immediately – also electronically. An essential argument was – that has come from the ÖVP, in particular from colleague Gerstl – that people often find it difficult to go to their own community. It is easier to go to another community and support a popular desire there. That is so, you can say what you want, whether it follows an immediate consequence or not. It is often not pleasant for people, and we know this from our own experience for many years, even when popular desire was “Austria first” it was very unpleasant for people – to judge the reports we had – to be crazy. Of course, it's not going in the direction that you're actually saying that you haven't got a kindergarten for that reason, that doesn't tell you, but at least that's very unpleasant. Not least, it was a real argument of the ÖVP to say that we want to be able to support popular desires everywhere. I am also of this opinion, but therefore I do not understand why it should be a problem to be able to make a statement of support for a choice in another community. Technically, this would be just as possible. I think it's really about not wanting to make it too easy – that's what happened in the discussion: you should not make it too easy – but then you should be so honest and say: All right, you have to lift the obstacles! One does not want too many candidate groups, perhaps too many parties in Parliament, because one is afraid that the country is no longer eligible for government. If that is the case, then you should say it openly, and then you should consider putting up the obstacles to support signatures, then you can discuss them. To find an artificial arrangement here and to say that one can support popular desires, but not a candidate party, that is not justified for me objectively. That is why we will agree with this additional proposal from the NEOS. [[I should like to congratulate the FPÖ and the representatives of the NEOS.]] Of course, this is a long way from the end. It has also been mentioned: We have massive problems in the right of election, and the essential problem is and remains the choice of letters – you can now say what you want; I say this very often – because the principles of election are not guaranteed in the election of the letter – that is once the very basic – but it is simply and simply problems in the implementation. We've had the subject of the Kuverts. Meanwhile, we are drawing back on the old Kuverts, which used to be there for electoral cards. These old Kuverts, however, have a major data protection problem because one can finally understand who voted. We are doing this for pragmatic reasons so that, if there is an advanced national council election, we are able to make the choice at all. The choice of letters continues to be the problem. The order of a postcard – we have often criticized this here –: It is simple and easy to order a postcard by e-mail for someone else. I only need to enter his passport data or ID data. The identification data is very easy, you can read it from the base book, from documents and so on. We have always criticized that. Now it turns out – and I also know that this is possible – that these identification data will not be tested or will only be tested in a sample way. There you can now say what you want – if someone claims something else – it has turned out right now, journalists have obviously carried out this provocation. I know that only samples are tested. If I read – that still needs to be clarified – that a obviously private institution, namely electoral card proposal.at, should organize these electoral cards and apparently control identity data, then I wonder how these data come to be. [[Abg. New builder: Unmistakable!]] How do they get the ID data? Or they didn't have it, so they couldn't control it. It's completely unclear to me. This has changed. I know that vote-cards proposal.at now refers only to the communities themselves, practically forwards the request only, so now is completely pointless, because everyone can do so anyway, to turn to the municipality of Vienna or to another community as well. It has to be clarified. There are just all these problems with the electoral cards. I will also insist that we make a real electoral right reform, with which we reduce the electoral cards to the necessary extent. It is a problem of this variety: If you choose 10, 15, 20 percent of the electorate or more with your choice of letter, then all these problems that we have will be possible. We know that there is usually no evidence because no one dares to really stand down then, but we always have very credible evidence that it has just happened that for several people election cards have been ordered that they were then filled together in clubs, in organizations. These clues are there; I don't have to say who that is. We must stop all of this, we must not be. We want to carry out proper elections, pan-free and in compliance with all the electoral principles, and I therefore hope that everyone here will be interested in it and that you will follow our very strong arguments as well. [[Congratulations at the FPÖ.]] One last point: This register of voters was also a small part of a further development of direct democracy. That should have been it. We have now improved a tiny area, namely that people's desires are easier to support. This is, of course, gratifying, but, of course, not what we understand by further development of direct democracy. We'll stick to that too. I hope you've learned from all these problems now. I am convinced that I know that the Minister of the Interior seriously believes that, of course, he has an interest in holding elections properly – that is not a question – and that he is also trying to do so, but I hope that the view will come where the problem lies, and that we will do so together. In any event, we agree today, but only from the point of view that there is, in particular, the implementation of pan-free, proper elections, in which all the principles of election are respected. Only then will we be truly satisfied.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e8262	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Ladies and gentlemen! There is a proposal for a legislative amendment which, in fact, has the subject of reorganising the salary reform, which entered into force in February 2015. Let me predict that I understand that there is a considerable need for questions in the context of the legal reorganisation of the remuneration reform 2015. We are dealing with a complex issue that has been with us for many years now and is related to issues of age discrimination. ==References====External links== On 9 September 2016, the Administrative Court of Justice issued a statement that the 2015 remuneration reform rules were illegal and not applicable. To the history I can briefly carry out the following: In line with the European Anti-Discrimination Directive of 2000, the European Court of Justice in the case of Hütter in 2009 recognised that pre-service accounting in the public service is age discrimination. He did so because he was training. Dung- und Schulzeite after the 18th century. In the public service, years of life have always been recognized as pre-services and training and schooling before the 18th century. years of life were not counted as pre-service periods. In the course of the 2015 salary reform, an attempt was made to comply with the requirements of the European Court of Justice. What did you do? – A new salary system has been created to which all the federal officials have been transferred, and the actual salary has been reduced and no recalculation has been carried out. In this reform, these old, age-discriminating provisions have been put out of effect and declared non-applicable. This time, however, the Administrative Court held that the legislator would be understood differently and that the legislator could not have meant that the old provisions were no longer applicable because otherwise salary components of the past – before February 2015 – would not be verifiable. It concludes in its knowledge that, in the immediate application of Union law, the staff must therefore be counted three years at pre-service times, because they are bringing the old right back to life. This would result in a massive budgetary burden, and in order to avoid these financial consequences, we have very quickly developed a reorganisation. Now we are facing the situation that we are indeed rehabilitating the 2015 salary reform. We have very much addressed the arguments of the findings of the Administrative Court and have also tried to close the regulatory gap which the Administrative Court takes up. In this reorganisation, we make it clear that the old provisions without room for interpretation are not explicitly applicable to future and ongoing procedures. The aim of this regulation is to avoid a significant financial burden on the Republic, which is why we have worked so quickly, without any losses on individual staff. We had to react very quickly with this bill because otherwise there would have been a disparity between staff in the future. The situation is very difficult, and I can ask you, ladies and gentlemen, to understand and to agree to this regulation. – Thank you very much.

I do not want to stop with past or present reforms of the right of remuneration – much has already been said about this – but rather I would like to address the future of public service service and remuneration. The current government programme provides for the modernisation of service law in the Modern State chapter. The aim is to create a modern, independent and uniform service law with specific features of the profession. The aim is to take into account, on the one hand, the requirements of public welfare in order to ensure optimum performance for citizens as well as for businesses with a basic legal orientation, and, on the other hand, to ensure the rule of law in a comprehensive manner, the new law on services must also be appropriate. Finally, modern pay procedures are intended to ensure the competitiveness of the service provider's federal government in the labour market also for the future. In order to achieve this objective, a level playing field is to be prepared which includes a more uniform and flatter remuneration structure for future contract staff and officials. In addition, the level building of the new service law should be designed in such a way as to take into account job specificities. What a modernisation of service law can look like, for example, shows us individual federal states such as Styria, Lower Austria and Upper Austria. This has also been a positive observation to the Court of Auditors. At the federal level, we have the above-mentioned objectives and the measures to achieve the objective according to the government programme, which, however, is still lacking, is implementation. There's nothing to add to that. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e9003	We're coming to 4. Agenda item. First word message: Mr Spindelberger. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e9028	Mr President. I was convinced that Mr Rasinger would have voted on this side as well. At the beginning, however, I would like to express my real joy at the fact that you, Minister of State, Oberhauser, have found the way to us. This shows me that you are back on the path of improvement and can devote yourself to health care again. I'm really happy! [[General condolences.]] My contribution is that it was once again necessary by EU directives to adapt the Austrian legislation, in a specific case the tissue safety law. On the one hand, this is an import directive, and this Directive states that the 13 tissue banks currently authorised in Austria, which import cells and tissues from abroad, better said, from third countries, need not only an authorisation from the Federal Office for Safety in Health, but also an obligation on these tissue banks. to conclude the contracts with third country suppliers in full detail, in order to ensure that these European high values are ensured in third countries as well. In addition to this import directive, it is also about the so-called coding directive. There will again be many who are not concerned with matter: What's that? – This is the use of a mandatory single European code in the import and export of human tissues and cells to ensure that traceability is facilitated within the European Union from donor to recipient and vice versa. For this purpose, the EU Commission shall provide a publicly accessible coding platform with a tissue facility register and a product register containing all types of tissues and tissue products on the market in the EU with the appropriate codes. And although in the past, if I can follow the annual report of the Federal Office for Safety in Health, there has been hardly any serious incidents in Austria, this legal change in my opinion leads to even more safety for patients who, for example, have to undergo genetic therapy. In my view, this is really an important project, which is why I ask all of you to agree to this bill.

The motion for a resolution, which is here today for decision, is pleased to be supported by the opposition. With the case law of the European Court of Justice, a revision of the Pharmacopoeia Act is brought about. This, in turn, improves the performance of pharmacies. The authority is now able to have a pharmacy installed even if the number 5 500 of the persons to be provided is lower. So it's possible. Further pharmacies will be installed if special conditions exist, such as increased demand at airports or if several ambulances are available. It can also be better provided for rural areas, and I am particularly pleased to be a local speaker. The relevant passage in the legislative text reads: “on the basis of special local conditions in the interest of proper care for the population. ” This also opens up the possibility of installing additional pharmacies in rural areas. It is also expected that primary supply centres will be created. So there will be several doctors in smaller space, and this will mean that there will also be several medications needed there. It is true that people with reduced mobility can also use the nearby pharmacy, buy or get their medicines there exactly where they are with the doctor. But if you are a person with disabilities, it looks completely different. The desire for their own four walls, for an autonomous, self-determined life, simply fails in Austria very often because of the non-existent possibilities for people with disabilities. In fact, it is still the rule that people with disabilities should live in specified facilities. Often these housing facilities are also at the same time the workplace of people with disabilities or their treatment place. This is a fact that we all probably cannot imagine, because we just go from apartment to work. This is important for a fulfilled and self-determined life. The fact that there are still very many housing facilities for people with disabilities actually contradicts the UN Convention on the Rights of People with Disabilities – in Article 19 of this is a self-determined life. In my view, this is a condition that would be easy to change, especially with regard to the self-determination of people with disabilities. The National Disability Action Plan sets out the objectives of de-institutionalisation – the UN National Report does not give a satisfactory witness to Austria – and we still need some follow-up to ensure it, especially as regards the need for assistance to people with disabilities. That is why I am sorry that the financial compensation has failed to do this inclusion fund with money. I think we should work on this together. There are also good examples in Austria of alternative forms of housing for people with disabilities. In this proposal, which we will decide together today – and in the Committee the decision was unanimous – the Federal Minister for Foreign Affairs will be invited to: In the framework of the NAP follow-up group, calls on the countries to establish a mutual To compare and determine the alternative forms of housing for people with disabilities and what can be learned from the best in order to make this autonomous, self-determined housing a matter of course for people with disabilities, for example, so that people with disabilities can telephone whenever they want it, and not go to sleep when someone else tells them, but when they decide for themselves. In this respect, I would like to ask you all to agree to this very important request. - Thank you.

This proposal concerns accompanying research and not yet concrete implementation, so we are only taking the initial steps. It works like this: Pensioners are, of course, insured. 5.1 percent of the pension is transferred to health insurance companies, which retains the pension insurance, the respective institution, directly. In addition to these 5.1 percent, a so-called lifting rate is transferred by the pension fund to the health insurance institution. But it's always different: As a result, the pension insurance institution has to transfer 180 percent, the social insurance institution of the industrial economy 197 percent, the insurance institution for railways and mining 310 percent and the agricultural social security scheme 397 percent. These lifting rates are laid down by Parliament and incorporated into the law. Only there are no parameters for why one insurance is 190 percent, the other 310 percent and the third 397 percent. No one can tell you where that comes from and why it is, that's what you've decided sometime. This is another example of the cross-financing flows and the transparency of the Austrian social security system. ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e12935	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Being sick is never pleasant. It is therefore important for people to know that one can count on a health system, which then brings good medical care, if it is really necessary, and that it does not depend on whether one has money on the bank account or has to spend other financial resources on it. Austria has a solidary health system that can provide the best possible medical care to all people, no matter what social shifts they come from. The Austrian social security system is based on the principle of compulsory insurance, solidarity and self-management and is financed mainly by insurance contributions. Solidarity is also achieved in a balance between vulnerable and less vulnerable persons, such as high-income and low-income groups. Contributions from active contribution periods are usually fed to almost equal portions of service providers and employees' contributions. Currently, workers pay 3.87 percent and companies 3.78 percent, so total 7.65 percent. Pensioners are legally insured. The majority of them are entitled to a pension under the General Social Security Act and are paying 5.1 percent of the gross pension – which has already been mentioned today. The contributions are transferred by the social security funds to the health insurance companies. Since pensioners do not pay a service provider's contribution, the Federal Government pays into the system a fictitious servicer's share calculated by means of health insurance with the lifting rate. The solidarity community is thus financing this health insurance for pensioners. The pension insurance institutions are responsible for this. Therefore, the different contribution that has been made today is also interesting insofar as, of course, any insurance in its structure is different, the relationship between the active and pensioners in each institution is different. Insurance institutions with more pensioners have a little different problems than insurance institutions with very many active contributors. Some insurance institutions are therefore setting up reserves, which are mainly used for benefits if you have problems within the insurance institution itself. It is therefore important to me to stress here that the issue of the setting up of reserves in the insurance institutions should not be seen negatively, but that it is in principle important and necessary to be able to rely on reserves as well. The issue raised earlier in connection with the farmer's insurance institution is, of course, a problem of its own, and I do not, of course, necessarily see it as a positive step towards dealing with these setbacks, and I am aware that farmers in Austria are currently in a very difficult situation. However, it should be discussed whether other support would not be possible. My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen, the discussion on the reduction of social security funds is being brought here again and again. The fact is that social insurance companies provide an excellent performance for their insured persons. For a long time, mergers and reductions in insurance companies do not mean that you are better and more economical. Therefore: Put numbers and facts on the table step by step, then you can talk about many things. Thank you.

How did the lifting sets come about? – First of all: In the main version of the ASVG there has already been a differentiated way of dealing with the health insurance of pensioners. So there has already been something in the ASVG’s original version, which has taken into account the relationship between those who are active contributors and those who are out of the pension. The Bundesgesetzblatt 110 from 1993 introduced the following rates: the financing of health insurance funds and the relationship between active contributors and pensioners on the one hand and pensioners on the other. For some insurance companies, it is indeed true that, due to economic changes, for example, in agriculture, the ratio of active contributors is due to and a high number of people are retired. This is, of course, an important point in financing, and I have answered that. You're right: There is no mathematical model of how high the lifting sets are to be, but the lifting sets are legitimated by the decision of the National Council. A mathematical model is not based on this. In the Federal Government, we have discussed the question of whether we can provide the farmers' social security institution with one of the quarterly contributions – or whatever we want to deal with the financial needs. Of course, to address this critically is justified. But I think it is important to help farmers if they have economic problems. The question of what is the appropriate instrument will continue to concern us in the next few days. I have invited agriculture and its representatives to propose how a repayment model could have been designed after a support. Mr Loacker, on the extent to which health insurance is to form reserves, the ASVG has adopted a scheme. It requires health insurance companies to have a three-month deposit, so they are to make a benefit deposit of the amount that the insurance benefits amount to for three months. If contributions allow one to make reserves beyond that, it is legitimate to think about whether lifting sets are too low or too high. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e14371	Next speaker: Mr Hell. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e14393	Madam President. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! I should like to comment briefly on Mr Loacker's motion for a resolution on voluntary legislation. In principle, the applicant recognises in the voluntary year of integration a suitable instrument to better integrate refugees into our society. There are still some legal measures required. This includes a pocket money not to be charged to minimum security for asylum seekers, an extension of the area of application to municipalities, the charging of continuing vocational training offers and the facilitation of the requirement for the institutions. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen! Asylum seekers have been able to complete a voluntary year of integration since 2016. This voluntary year of integration aims to improve job opportunities for asylum seekers and to bring integration into Austrian society. It is not an employment relationship, it is comparable to the voluntary social year, i.e. a mixture of training relationship and volunteering. This voluntary year of integration can be offered to all those organisations which are recognised by decision and civil service bodies, which are therefore also able to deal with this organisationally and qualitatively. Participants in this voluntary year of integration will have a need-oriented minimum guarantee. The law currently does not provide any pocket money, but it is free for the carriers to pay out pocket money. Ladies and gentlemen! I am convinced that with the introduction of the voluntary year of integration, Austria offers asylum seekers an opportunity to participate in social life in order to acquire improved labour market opportunities for the future. I therefore see no need for us to amend this law here. – Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e14449	Madam President. Federal Minister! On the subject of modification of the Voluntary Act: Any change to the Voluntary Act should mean that recognised refugees, as well as eligible beneficiaries whose livelihood is financed by the minimum guarantee, should receive compensation in a voluntary year as a preparation for the integration into the labour market for social benefits, which should not be offset by the minimum guarantee. The purpose of this measure is to contribute to integration. However, from my point of view, this request must be rejected as follows: Approved refugees and eligible beneficiaries are entitled to minimum protection without ever having been paid into the social system. The domestic labour market is characterised by long-term, high unemployment, and thus the preparation of foreigners for the labour market is a disadvantage to the unemployed. As long as local unemployed people are unable to successfully integrate into the labour market, the involvement of foreigners is not understandable. In addition to linguistic deficits, consideration should also be given to their technical qualification. Although considerable support is provided by the Chamber of Commerce, which undertakes to employ recognised refugees, their chances in the labour market are considered to be low. The right to minimum security shall be considered to be at least minor activities without further payment. In my opinion, this would be the better way. The request is therefore to be rejected from my point of view. Thank you.

At this point, it could well be considered for the next legislative period to include the media in the Art and Cultural Committee, where it is actually a matter of substance. This is also the case in many other countries, and this area is ultimately also covered by the same Minister. That's a little off topic. But the pure amount of funding in the art/media field does not create excellence, does not create any innovation either. I remember quite well that two years ago I happened to meet my friend Tina Leisch, who is a director, on a train trip from Vienna to Innsbruck. Tina Leisch is one of the few established women in the film industry. On this trip she then told me how difficult it is to have a place as a woman in the film industry at all, starting from the funding funds to the project applications. I don't want to advertise the ÖBB now, but you really come up with new ideas on such a train trip, and Tina Leisch has taught me the idea to really make me smart in the film industry, to see if it really is. I then tried, after this four-hour journey, as a non-expert person, to make a thorough investigation into whether it really is, and I have come to believe that there really is hardly a professor at the Vienna Film Academy, and I have then noticed that the most important decision-making bodies in the field of funding organizations have been involved. who are very malely dominated. I also thought that there was only a small percentage of women in domestic production. This was not necessarily for me as a politician, as a woman’s speaker, but primarily for me as a woman, because there is a system that simply works unfairly. This is not an issue that should only be of interest to women, but I believe that it is a social issue. I then noticed that there are not only legal gaps, but also hardly any policy measures at this level, and I have tried to really deal with the matter in concrete terms. I did that with the help of my colleague Eva Mückstein. Her daughter Katharina Mückstein is a director who is involved in a representation of interests, FC Gloria, where women have been working for years for fair structures in the film industry. After a discussion with the interest representative FC Gloria, we developed a request that I presented to my colleagues in the equal treatment committee. I really must say that my colleague, Mr Worm and Mr Schittenhelm, have immediately participated, have shown themselves very solid, have really taken care that we can create something common from the whole thing. As a result, of course, the request has also ended up in the Committee on Culture. Mr Zinggl and I have then tried to make this request even more powerful, even more concrete. In Sweden, there are already more just structures in the film industry. There is a quota system, so one looks at the fact that no sex gets more than 60 percent of the funding. And that's just a sign we should finally see. I mean, we've already checked it, but I hope that the colleagues from the FPÖ really do understand that on a voluntary basis you simply can't create just structures. Sometimes it takes concrete measures, sometimes it takes a quota system to enable the overqualified good women, unlike the average qualified men, to be rewarded according to their qualifications. That is why the quota system is very important. Now again to my request: I am very happy and very proud that we have done something common, but I see this as a partial success; only being proud of the request is too little for me. I see this only as the beginning of a period for women in the film industry, in which we can get even more in this regard. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2016-11-10-025-XXV-NRSITZ-00152_d7e16991	Mr President. Only recently has the theme become virulent of the possibility of recording audio and images in surveys. There was the CETA survey, with the participation of Commissioner Malmström, and there was a public interest. However, due to the current provisions of the Rules of Procedure, it was not easy to ensure that there were images and recordings. Our idea is: Let's do this relatively transparently! Let us turn this around and make a regulation of the Rules of Procedure, according to which images and recordings of surveys are permitted in principle! Only in specific cases – there may be reasons – if another decision is taken can it be removed. In the last debate it was so that everyone said afterwards: Actually, everyone would have wanted it to be transferred, but unfortunately it didn't. That is why we want to resolve this in the framework of the Rules of Procedure. I think a survey is of great interest and can be mediated about it. The journalists have also turned to us – probably also to other Members – and said: This is actually absurd, Mrs Malmström speaks inside, we must not record it, and when the club people speak, we have to make extra recordings outside because there must be no transmission. Transparency would be good, and we want to make surveys publicly available in the future.
